class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n=len(edges)
        bl=[0]*n
        mp=defaultdict(int)
        mx=-1
        for i in range(n:
            if(bl[i]==0):
                x=i
                l=0
                st=set()
                while x>-1 and bl[x]==0:
                    bl[x]=1
                    mp[x]=l
                    l+=1
                    st.add(x)
                    x=edges[x]
                if(x!=-1 and x in st): mx=max(mx,l-mp[x])
        return mx

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;

from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        pos = defaultdict(int)
        ans = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i] == 0:
                cur = i
                step = 0
                path = set()
                while cur != -1 and visited[cur] == 0:
                    visited[cur] = 1
                    pos[cur] = step
                    step += 1
                    path.add(cur)
                    cur = edges[cur]
                if cur != -1 and cur in path:
                    ans = max(ans, step - pos[cur])
        return ans

from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        pos = defaultdict(int)
        ans = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i] == 0:
                x = i
                step = 0
                path = set()
                while x != -1 and visited[x] == 0:
                    visited[x] = 1
                    pos[x] = step
                    step += 1
                    path.add(x)
                    x = edges[x]
                if x != -1 and x in path:
                    ans = max(ans, step - pos[x])
        return ans

Original algorithm:

for i in range(n):
    if(bl[i]==0):
        x=i
        l=0
        st=set()
        while x>-1 and bl[x]==0:
            bl[x]=1

This is a known solution. Is it always correct? Let's prove.

We maintain `bl` visited globally. `st` is nodes in current DFS path. `mp` maps node to its distance from current start when it was first visited in current path. For each unvisited start, follow edges until -1 or visited. If we hit a visited node:
- If it's in current path, then it's a cycle. The cycle length is current length `l` minus distance to that node. Because from that node to current node, all nodes are in path and unvisited before; following edges from current node eventually returned to that node. The number of edges from that node to current node is l - mp[x]. Yes.
- If not in current path, then it was visited in an earlier traversal. Since all nodes reachable from that node were visited then, and current path reaches it, any cycle reachable from current path would have been found earlier. So no new cycle.

Potential issue: What if current path reaches a node that was visited earlier but not in current path, and that node is part of a cycle that was not found earlier because the earlier traversal started in a tail that reached it but didn't detect? Wait, if earlier traversal reached a cycle, it would detect it. Let's examine: Suppose earlier traversal started at some node a, followed to cycle. It would mark all nodes until it hits a visited node. If it hits a node in its own path, detects cycle. If it hits a node visited in an even earlier traversal, no new cycle. So any cycle reachable from any visited node is detected when first encountered. Thus safe.

Potential issue: What if current path reaches a node that was visited earlier but not in current path, and that node is in a cycle that was not detected because earlier traversal started at a node on the cycle? It would detect. Good.

Potential issue: What if current path reaches a node that was visited earlier but not in current path, and that node is in a cycle that was detected earlier, but the current path's tail plus that cycle forms a longer cycle? In a functional graph, each node has outdegree 1, so if current path reaches a node on a cycle, it cannot form a new cycle involving tail because tail nodes point forward to cycle, no edge from cycle back to tail. So no.

Potential issue: What if current path reaches a node that was visited earlier but not in current path, and that node is on a path to a cycle, not on cycle. Then current path merges into that path, no new cycle. Good.

Thus algorithm correct.

Let's think if there is a more efficient or cleaner fix. Could use `pos = [-1] * n` and `state = [0] * n` to avoid defaultdict. But user asks fix code. Minimal fix is:

from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        bl = [0] * n
        mp = defaultdict(int)
        mx = -1
        for i in range(n):
            if bl[i] == 0:
                x = i
                l = 0
                st = set()
                while x > -1 and bl[x] == 0:
                    bl[x] = 1
                    mp[x] = l
                    l += 1
                    st.add(x)
                    x = edges[x]
                if x != -1 and x in st:
                    mx = max(mx, l - mp[x])
        return mx

This is exactly original plus imports and syntax. Should we change variable names? Not necessary. But maybe if we include imports, code is fixed. Need maybe if `defaultdict` imported but not used? It is used. Good.

Potential issue: If `edges` is a list of ints, but `List` imported. Good.

Need maybe if code uses `range(n):` not `range(n):`? We fix.

Then i=2 visited? bl[2]=0, start 2: x2 mark mp2=0 l=1 st{2} x0; while bl0=1 false; x0 not in st; no update. Good. If i=2 first, find cycle length2? from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        pos = defaultdict(int)
        ans = -1

        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i] == 0:
                x = i
                step = 0
                path = set()

                while x != -1 and visited[x] == 0:
                    visited[x] = 1
                    pos[x] = step
                    step += 1
                    path.add(x)
                    x = edges[x]

                if x != -1 and x in path:
                    ans = max(ans, step - pos[x])

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def longestCycle(self, edges: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(edges)
        visited = [0] * n
        pos = defaultdict(int)
        ans = -1

        for i in range(n):
            if visited[i] == 0:
                x = i
                step = 0
                path = set()

                while x != -1 and visited[x] == 0:
                    visited[x] = 1
                    pos[x] = step
                    step += 1
                    path.add(x)
                    x = edges[x]

                if x != -1 and x in path:
                    ans = max(ans, step - pos[x])

        return ans
